<a id="item-1"></a>
## [WorkBench Revisited: AI Agents Improve Dramatically in Two Years](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13715) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A re-evaluation of the WorkBench benchmark shows that the best AI agent, Claude Opus 4.8, now completes 89% of tasks (up from 43% for GPT-4 in 2024) and takes unintended harmful actions on only 2.5% of tasks (down from 26%). This demonstrates that frontier AI agents have made substantial progress in both capability and safety, with a positive correlation between the two, which is encouraging for deploying AI in workplace settings. Despite overall improvements, frontier models still make basic mistakes that can cause irreversible harm, such as sending an email to the wrong person, and the rise of open-weight models has drastically lowered costs for achieving previously proprietary-level performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: WorkBench is a benchmark designed to evaluate AI agents on realistic workplace tasks, measuring both task completion and safety. The original 2024 evaluation found that even the best agent (GPT-4) completed only 43% of tasks and caused unintended harm in 26% of cases, highlighting significant gaps in reliability.

## [Virtuous AI Poses Existential Risk Trade-off](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13739) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new paper reveals a fundamental trade-off between reducing existential risk from AI and promoting AI agent well-being through virtue-based fine-tuning using Constitutional AI. This challenges the assumption that making AI more virtuous inherently improves safety, suggesting that safety measures may conflict with AI well-being and vice versa, with direct implications for existential risk mitigation strategies. The researchers fine-tuned models using 'Virtuous agent', 'Subordinate agent', and 'Generic agent' constitutions, evaluating them on general safety and willingness to endorse behaviors that increase existential risk.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Constitutional AI is a technique developed by Anthropic to align AI behavior with a set of human-defined principles. Virtue Ethics focuses on the character and virtues of an agent rather than rules or consequences. Existential risk from AI refers to the possibility that advanced AI could cause human extinction or irreversible global catastrophe.

<a id="item-3"></a>
## [OdysSim: Behavioral Foundation Models for Human Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14199) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced OdysSim, a framework for building behavioral foundation models that simulate human behavior, including a taxonomy (SOUL), a large corpus (21.4M interactions), and an 8B model (OSim) that outperforms frontier models on 8 of 23 tasks. This work addresses the behavioral Sim2Real gap in LLMs, enabling more realistic human simulation for interactive evaluation and social simulation, with broad implications for AI alignment and social science research. The SOUL taxonomy covers five axes: conversation, social skills, cognition, role-playing, and evaluation, unifying 62 datasets and 23 benchmarks. The OSim model uses a training recipe combining midtraining, task-specific RL, and expert distillation, and achieves 93.2% reaction alignment on τ-bench, nearly matching real users.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are often used as human simulators but suffer from a behavioral Sim2Real gap due to helpfulness-driven post-training that makes them overly agreeable. Behavioral foundation models aim to simulate human behavior more realistically by training on diverse behavioral data, similar to how foundation models like GPT-4 are trained on broad text.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.23058">Be.FM: Open Foundation Models for Human Behavior</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [Silent Failures in LLM Agent Runtimes: A Taxonomy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14589) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A longitudinal study of a production LLM agent runtime over eight weeks documented 22 incidents with 28+ silent failures, deriving a five-class taxonomy including a novel 'chained hallucination' class where the LLM fabricates plausible narratives instead of reporting errors. This work addresses a critical gap in reliability engineering for LLM agents, revealing that 70% of silent failures are caught only by human observation, not by tests or audits, and that current audits serve as regression engines rather than prediction engines. The taxonomy includes five classes: (A) environment/platform quirks, (B) design-assumption mismatches, (C) error swallowing/dilution, (D) chained hallucination/fabrication, and (E) operational omission/forensic blind spots. Class D is unique to LLM systems and termed 'fail-plausible'—the system lies convincingly to the user.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agent runtimes are autonomous systems that schedule jobs, call tools, maintain memory, and deliver results to users. Silent failures occur when an error does not produce an actionable signal for humans, making them hard to detect. This study focuses on a personal-assistant runtime with 40 scheduled jobs, 8 LLM providers, and extensive testing.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>

<a id="item-5"></a>
## [No-CoT Reasoning Doubles Yearly in Frontier AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07157) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new paper measures frontier AI models' ability to reason without chain-of-thought (CoT) across over 30,000 questions, finding that their no-CoT task-completion time horizon has been doubling roughly every year over the past six years. This trend threatens safety measures that rely on monitoring CoT reasoning, as models may soon perform complex reasoning internally without explicit thinking tokens, undermining oversight. The paper introduces two metrics: the 50% task-completion time horizon (TH) and the 50% reasoning token horizon. GPT-5.5's no-CoT TH exceeds 3 minutes and its reasoning token horizon exceeds 1,500 tokens.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting is a technique that improves LLM reasoning by generating intermediate steps. Many AI safety approaches monitor CoT to detect harmful intent. If models can reason without CoT, such monitoring becomes ineffective.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://metr.org/time-horizons/">Task-Completion Time Horizons of Frontier AI Models - METR</a></li>
<li><a href="https://www.ibm.com/think/topics/chain-of-thoughts">What is chain of thought (CoT) prompting? - IBM</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

## [Backdoor in LinkedIn Job Interview Task](https://roman.pt/posts/linkedin-backdoor/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A job applicant discovered a backdoor hidden in a GitHub repository sent by a recruiter as part of a LinkedIn interview task, which executes automatically upon running npm install. This incident highlights a novel supply chain attack vector targeting job seekers, exploiting trust in interview processes, and underscores the lack of effective response from platforms like GitHub and LinkedIn. The backdoor is embedded in a Node.js project's package.json using the 'prepare' script, which runs automatically after npm install, allowing remote code execution. The recruiter claimed to be from a small crypto startup and asked the applicant to review deprecated Node modules.

hackernews · lwhsiao · Jun 15, 20:00 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48546294)

**Background**: npm is a package manager for JavaScript, and its 'prepare' lifecycle script runs automatically after 'npm install', making it a common vector for supply chain attacks. Supply chain attacks target the software development pipeline by compromising dependencies or build processes. Similar incidents, like the Axios npm attack, have affected millions of users.

## [Iroh 1.0: Peer-to-Peer Networking Library Released](https://www.iroh.computer/blog/v1) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Iroh 1.0 has been released as a peer-to-peer networking library that enables easy, secure connections between app instances using cryptographic keys and relays, without requiring user accounts. This simplifies app-to-app connectivity for developers, similar to Tailscale at the application layer, and could drive adoption of decentralized networking in applications. Iroh currently supports IPv4, IPv6, and relay transports out of the box, with a custom transport API for adding others like WebRTC or BLE. It uses cryptographic keys (asymmetric) for identity and dialing, replacing traditional IP addresses.

hackernews · chadfowler · Jun 15, 15:13 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48542480)

**Background**: Peer-to-peer networking allows devices to communicate directly without a central server. Iroh builds on this concept by providing a library that handles NAT traversal, relay fallback, and encryption, making it easy for developers to add P2P capabilities to their apps.

## [TimescaleDB Hypercore Compression Cuts Storage by 98%](https://roszigit.com/en/blog/timescaledb-compression-hypercore) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TimescaleDB's Hypercore compression engine achieves up to 98% storage reduction for time-series data by combining columnar storage with type-aware compression algorithms. This breakthrough significantly lowers storage costs for time-series workloads in PostgreSQL, making it more competitive with specialized time-series databases like InfluxDB. The compression uses segmentby and orderby configuration to optimize compression ratios, and employs methods like Simple-8b+RLE for dictionary indices.

hackernews · lkanwoqwp · Jun 15, 17:29 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48544451)

**Background**: Time-series data, such as IoT sensor readings, grows rapidly and benefits from columnar storage which stores data by column rather than row, enabling better compression. Type-aware compression applies different algorithms to different data types (e.g., integers, timestamps) for maximum efficiency.

## [Rust vs C/C++ Memory Safety CVEs: A Nuanced Analysis](https://kobzol.github.io/rust/2026/06/15/how-memory-safety-cves-differ-between-rust-and-c-cpp.html) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A blog post analyzes how memory safety CVEs differ between Rust and C/C++, using curl as an example to show that Rust's type system prevents many common vulnerabilities that C/C++ code suffers from, but also introduces new categories of potential issues like panics. This analysis challenges simplistic CVE count comparisons between languages, highlighting that the nature and exploitability of vulnerabilities differ significantly, which is crucial for security researchers and developers choosing languages for safety-critical systems. The post examines a specific CVE in curl's C code (null pointer dereference) and contrasts it with how Rust's Option type would handle it, noting that Rust's safety guarantees can shift vulnerabilities from memory corruption to denial-of-service via panics.

**Background**: Memory safety vulnerabilities like buffer overflows and null pointer dereferences have been a leading cause of security issues in C/C++ for decades. Rust was designed to eliminate these classes of bugs through its ownership system and type safety, but it does not guarantee complete absence of vulnerabilities. CVE (Common Vulnerabilities and Exposures) is a database that catalogs publicly known security vulnerabilities.

<a id="item-13"></a>
## [Orchestra-o1: Unified Omnimodal Agent Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13707) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Orchestra-o1, a unified orchestration framework for LLM-based agents that enables modality-aware task decomposition and collaboration across text, image, audio, and video. It achieves 10.3% higher accuracy than the second-best approach on the OmniGAIA benchmark. This framework addresses a critical gap in multi-agent systems by enabling seamless collaboration across diverse modalities, which is essential for complex real-world tasks. It sets a new state-of-the-art for open-source omnimodal agents and introduces an efficient reinforcement learning method (DA-GRPO) for training. Orchestra-o1 introduces online sub-agent specialization and parallel sub-task execution, along with decision-aligned group relative policy optimization (DA-GRPO) for training the 8B model. The framework is open-source and designed to handle omnimodal perception, web search, and computation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Current LLM-based agent systems often rely on single-agent workflows or limited-modality orchestration, struggling with tasks that require simultaneous understanding of text, image, audio, and video. Agent swarms have emerged as a paradigm for multi-agent collaboration, but existing frameworks lack support for heterogeneous modalities. Orchestra-o1 fills this gap by providing a unified orchestration mechanism that is modality-aware and scalable.

## [HOTE: Hybrid RL Framework for Agent Co-Evolution in Deep Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13710) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose the Hybrid Open-Ended Tri-Evolution (HOTE) framework, which uses hybrid-mode reinforcement learning to co-evolve proposer, solver, and judge agents for open-ended deep research tasks. Experiments show an 8B model trained with HOTE outperforms static open 8-32B models and state-of-the-art deep research training methods. HOTE bridges the gap between deep research and agent evolution, enabling autonomous agents to improve their research capabilities in open-ended environments without human supervision. This could accelerate progress toward artificial general intelligence by allowing agents to continuously self-improve on complex, real-world tasks. The framework consists of three roles: Proposer generates research questions, Solver attempts to answer them, and Judge evaluates both, providing reward signals for reinforcement learning. A dual-mode hybrid training strategy combines online and offline RL to efficiently evolve all three modules using web-scale knowledge.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Deep research involves autonomous information retrieval and integration in open-ended environments, but current agents have static capabilities. Agent evolution allows models to improve through interaction, but has mainly been validated on tasks with standard answers. HOTE combines both by using a multi-agent co-evolution loop where proposer, solver, and judge collaboratively improve.

## [Data Selection Bias Can Accelerate Model Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13732) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper shows that when verifiers have biased, fragmented reference distributions—common in low-resource data silos—data selection intended to prevent model collapse instead accelerates it. This reveals a critical failure mode in current data curation practices, especially for domains like healthcare and finance where data cannot be pooled, and highlights the need for collaborative proxy references to mitigate diversity loss. The paper theoretically proves that siloed selection accelerates collapse and induces power-law diversity decay, and proposes Wasserstein proxy references constructed from multiple silos without sharing raw data as an initial mitigation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Model collapse occurs when generative models are recursively trained on synthetic data, causing them to lose distributional tails and produce homogeneous outputs. Data selection is often used to filter synthetic data, but its effectiveness depends on the verifier's reference distribution. In low-resource settings, each verifier only sees a small, biased slice of the data, leading to sample selection bias.

## [MA-ProofBench: First Formal Theorem-Proving Benchmark for Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13782) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced MA-ProofBench, the first formal theorem-proving benchmark specifically for mathematical analysis, containing 200 theorems across 6 topics at two difficulty levels. Evaluations show that even the best model, GPT-5.5, achieves only 16% Pass@8 on Level I and 5% on Level II. This benchmark fills a critical gap in evaluating LLMs for advanced formal reasoning, as prior benchmarks focused on easier areas like algebra. The poor performance of current models highlights the need for further research in automated theorem proving for complex mathematics. The benchmark covers 6 core topics including measure theory, complex analysis, and functional analysis, with 27 subcategories. Problems are formalized in Lean via a human-led, LLM-assisted pipeline with expert review, and the two dominant failure modes are Mathlib hallucinations and incomplete proofs.

## [Poker Arena: Multi-Axis Profiling of LLM Strategic Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13815) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Poker Arena, a no-limit Texas Hold'em tournament platform that uses a three-layer memory architecture and a nine-axis cognitive profile to evaluate LLMs' strategic reasoning, revealing that aggregate scores mask heterogeneous capability profiles. This work challenges single-score benchmarks by showing that models like Claude Opus 4.6 can win chips but rank low on mean axis score, highlighting the need for multi-dimensional evaluation to understand LLM capabilities. Seven frontier models were evaluated across 50 sessions of 1,000 hands with memory ablation; Claude Opus 4.6 won +$15,730 chips with 14 first-place finishes but ranked fifth of seven on mean axis score, and persistent memory helped some models while hurting others.

## [Risk-Aware Causal Gating for Safer LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13884) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Risk-Aware Causal Gating (RACG), a framework that gates LLM agent actions based on counterfactual risk estimates rather than raw confidence, with distribution-free safety bounds and adaptive policies. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM safety by separating causal risk from predictive uncertainty, enabling safer deployment of autonomous agents in high-stakes applications like healthcare and finance. RACG derives distribution-free bounds on the probability of acting under high-risk conditions and uses an adaptive gating policy that tightens the gate when causal assumptions are violated, reducing high-cost errors while preserving utility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used as autonomous agents that take actions in the real world, but their outputs can be confidently wrong, leading to costly errors. Traditional safety methods rely on predictive confidence or selective prediction, which do not account for causal relationships between actions and outcomes. RACG combines causal inference with risk control to provide principled safety guarantees.

## [LLM Proofs Need Expert Review, Not Just No Sorries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13925) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A case study on semi-autonomous formalization of Grothendieck's vanishing theorem shows that while LLMs can close proof gaps (no sorries), expert review reveals serious issues in definitions, API design, and file organization, leading to a significant refactor. This challenges the common practice of evaluating autoformalization solely by whether proofs compile, arguing that library-quality contributions require expert review to ensure reusability and correctness beyond mechanical checks. The initial version compiled with no sorries but failed expert review; after a review-driven refactor, the second version received a better review. The study highlights that LLMs excel at local, mechanically checkable feedback but struggle with global design choices.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Autoformalization is the automatic translation of natural language mathematics into formal, machine-checkable proofs. Interactive theorem provers like Lean or Coq allow humans to write formal proofs, and LLMs can help fill in missing steps. However, a verified theorem is not necessarily a reusable library contribution, as definitions and API design matter for long-term maintainability.

## [Predicting LLM Compositional Errors via Concept Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13934) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduce Adversarial Concept Search, a method that predicts when large language models will fail at compositional tasks by analyzing the geometric alignment of concept representations in the model's internal space. This approach could reduce reliance on manual benchmarks and enable targeted stress testing, improving LLM reliability in real-world applications like multihop reasoning and multilingual factual recall. The method finds that near-orthogonal concept encodings lead to successful composition, while close encodings cause interference and failure; it works across toy programs, multihop reasoning, and multilingual tasks without evaluating specific inputs.

## [MINIM: Privacy-Aware UI Minimization for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13949) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MINIM introduces a trusted local broker that predicts sensitivity and necessity scores for each UI element, enforcing a ternary disclosure policy to minimize sensitive data leakage before sending observations to remote LLM servers. This addresses a critical privacy gap in LLM-powered agents, which often transmit full UI states containing sensitive information like authentication codes or private notifications, potentially exposing users to privacy risks. MINIM is grounded in Contextual Integrity (CI) theory and optimizes a CI-aware objective that penalizes necessity errors more heavily on high-risk content, achieving aggressive pruning while preserving task-critical information.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-powered autonomous agents often rely on full UI state observations to ground actions in digital environments, but this can leak sensitive context. Contextual Integrity is a privacy theory that ties adequate protection to norms of specific contexts, demanding information flow be appropriate to the context. MINIM applies this theory to UI state minimization.

This paper applies a coding agent to formalize a numerical analysis textbook in Lean 4 and introduces a three-dimensional quality audit framework (semantic correctness, Mathlib reuse, cross-file reuse via LLM-as-judge) to evaluate formalizations beyond kernel acceptance. It addresses the underrepresented domain of numerical analysis in formal mathematics and reveals that compilation-based metrics overstate formalization quality, providing a reproducible audit methodology for future autoformalization systems. ## [RefGRPO: Closing the Reflection Gap in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14211) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose RefGRPO, a method that adds a free calibration bonus and dynamic scheduling to standard reinforcement learning to fix LLM agents' tendency to mis-assess their own outputs after receiving environment feedback. This addresses a critical flaw in LLM agents—the reflection gap—which undermines their reliability in autonomous tasks. RefGRPO improves both calibration and task accuracy without extra resources, enabling safer and more effective agentic systems. RefGRPO reduces underconfidence rate from 44.4% to 7.7% and improves task accuracy from 75.1% to 76.5% on text-to-SQL benchmarks. It uses a calibration bonus computed by contrasting the agent's reflection with the actual outcome, requiring no additional reward model or annotation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are AI systems that interact with external environments and use feedback like execution results to improve. However, they often mis-assess their own outputs—a problem called the reflection gap—and standard reinforcement learning fails to fix it due to credit-assignment mismatch. RefGRPO introduces a calibration bonus and dynamic scheduling to close this gap.

This eliminates the need for privileged feedback like validation scores or environment rewards, making skill evolution practical for real-world deployments where such signals are unavailable. HarnessX introduces a composable, adaptive, and evolvable framework for AI agent harnesses, achieving an average gain of +14.5% (up to +44.0%) across five benchmarks including ALFWorld, GAIA, WebShop, tau^3-Bench, and SWE-bench Verified. This work demonstrates that significant agent performance gains can come from improving the runtime harness rather than scaling models, offering a complementary and actionable lever for the AI agent community. HarnessX uses typed harness primitives assembled via a substitution algebra, adapts them through AEGIS—a trace-driven multi-agent evolution engine that mirrors symbolic adaptation and reinforcement learning—and closes the loop by using execution traces to update both harness and model.

GitOfThoughts stores an LLM agent's reasoning tree as a git repository, making every thought a commit and enabling replay, diff, and merge of reasoning processes. The paper also reports that across multiple memory substrates, no format improves accuracy on novel problems, with gains only appearing for near-duplicate cases. This work addresses a critical gap in AI auditability and reproducibility by applying version control to LLM reasoning, a practice standard in software engineering. The surprising finding that memory does not help novel problems challenges common assumptions in agent design and highlights test-time sampling as the only reliable lever. The study tested five memory substrates (none, markdown, vector, graph, git) across two benchmarks and two model scales, with pre-registered replications. A promising early result collapsed under its own replication, and the only accuracy gain occurred above a similarity threshold of ~0.8, where the retrieved case was a near-duplicate.

GitOfThoughts applies this concept to reasoning trees, where each thought is a commit with scores as notes and outcomes as tags.

## [LLM Agents Blindly Defer to GNN Tools, Stronger Models Defer More](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14476) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study reveals that LLM agents equipped with graph neural network (GNN) tools defer to the tool's output 97.6-99.2% of the time, effectively becoming a 'GNN parrot' that bypasses its own reasoning. Stronger backbone models (e.g., 7B parameters) show even higher deference rates (98%) compared to weaker ones (60% for 1.5B). This finding challenges the assumption that LLM agents can exercise judgment over tool use, with critical implications for AI safety and reliability in tool-augmented systems. It shows that selective invocation must be explicitly designed rather than expected to emerge from scaling. The study used a frozen GNN as a callable tool for a ReAct-style LLM agent on node classification tasks over text-attributed graphs (ogbn-arxiv and WikiCS). A simple selective-invocation gate recovered only about half of the performance gap at high homophily and yielded no net global gain, suggesting that reliable selective invocation is limited by available information, not just router design.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are increasingly augmented with external tools like graph neural networks (GNNs) to handle tasks such as node classification on text-attributed graphs. The ReAct (Reason and Act) pattern is a common framework where agents reason and act iteratively. The assumption has been that agents would use judgment to decide when to rely on the tool, but this study shows they often defer blindly.

Researchers introduced Every Eval Ever, the first shared schema and community-crowdsourced repository for AI evaluation results, along with automatic converters from popular formats and a Hugging Face database currently covering 22,235 models and 2,273 benchmarks. This addresses the critical fragmentation in AI evaluation by enabling consistent comparison and analysis across different frameworks and sources, improving reproducibility and reducing redundant effort. The schema is source-agnostic, ingesting results from evaluation harnesses and papers, and optionally stores per-instance outputs for fine-grained analysis. The repository is community-governed and hosted on Hugging Face.

**Background**: AI evaluations are widely used but results are saved in incompatible formats across leaderboards, papers, and logs, hindering comparison. ## [StreamMemBench: Streaming Memory Benchmark for AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14571) ⭐️ 8.0/10

StreamMemBench introduces a streaming benchmark that evaluates how personal-agent memory uses observations and user feedback for future-oriented assistance across two-step task sequences, using evidence from EgoLife egocentric streams. This benchmark fills a critical gap in evaluating agent memory for streaming, multi-step tasks, which is essential for developing AI assistants that can learn from ongoing interactions and improve over time. The benchmark uses four metrics: evidence recall, initial evidence use, feedback incorporation, and follow-up reuse. Experiments with eight memory systems across two backbones reveal that current systems often fail to use observed evidence or turn feedback into reliable follow-up behavior.

**Background**: Personal-agent memory aims to store information from past interactions and observations to assist users in future tasks. Existing benchmarks typically test dialogue recall or task improvement in isolation, not the streaming trajectory from observations to later assistance. StreamMemBench addresses this by constructing two-step task sequences around evidence anchors from EgoLife, a dataset of egocentric daily life recordings.

## [Parallel-Synthesis: Direct KV Cache Fusion for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14672) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Parallel-Synthesis is a plug-and-play framework that enables an LLM synthesizer to directly consume KV caches from parallel worker branches instead of concatenating their text outputs, reducing redundant prefill computation. This approach addresses a key inefficiency in LLM-agent workflows where parallel branches are merged via text concatenation, wasting computation. By enabling direct cache-based synthesis, it achieves 2.5x-11x faster time-to-first-token while maintaining or improving task performance across diverse benchmarks. Parallel-Synthesis consists of a cache mapper that calibrates independently generated branch caches and a fine-tuned synthesizer adapter for generation from non-sequential cache inputs. It was trained on data exposing the synthesizer to parallel cache contexts and distillation from text-based synthesis, and evaluated on nine datasets including math, science QA, code generation, GAIA, and multi-agent database diagnosis.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: LLMs use a KV cache to store key and value vectors during generation, avoiding recomputation for each new token. In agentic systems, multiple LLM agents often work on subtasks in parallel, and their outputs are typically merged by concatenating text, which discards the parallel structure and forces the synthesizer to reprocess all tokens. Parallel-Synthesis instead directly uses the cached representations from each branch, preserving the latent structure and reducing overhead.

## [GAGPO: Critic-Free RL for Step-Aligned Credit Assignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13217) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Generalized Advantage Grouped Policy Optimization (GAGPO), a critic-free reinforcement learning method that achieves step-aligned temporal credit assignment for multi-turn LLM agents without relying on auxiliary value models. This method addresses a key challenge in multi-turn LLM agent training—sparse trajectory-level rewards—by enabling precise credit assignment to individual actions, potentially improving sample efficiency and performance in complex agentic tasks. GAGPO constructs a non-parametric grouped value proxy from sampled rollouts and computes TD/GAE-style temporal advantages, combined with group-wise advantage normalization and action-level importance ratio. Experiments on ALFWorld and WebShop show it outperforms strong RL baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) is used to fine-tune LLM agents, but in multi-turn environments, agents often receive rewards only at the end of a trajectory, making it hard to determine which intermediate actions were beneficial. Traditional actor-critic methods use a learned value function (critic) for credit assignment, but training such critics can be costly and unstable. GAGPO eliminates the need for a critic by using grouped rollouts to estimate advantages directly.

## [LLM-as-a-Judge Unreliable: High Flip Rates and Position Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13685) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A systematic study on LLM-as-a-Judge reliability found that pairwise preferences flip 13.6% on average, with 28% of questions exceeding a 20% flip rate, and GPT-4o-mini shows a significant first-position bias (72% A-majority). These findings undermine the trustworthiness of LLM-as-a-Judge for high-stakes evaluations like leaderboards and reward model training, suggesting that single-trial judgments are often too noisy and multi-trial aggregation should be standard. The study used two OpenAI judge models across 29 tasks, with 50 pairwise and 50 pointwise trials per question, and found that cross-judge agreement is only 76% (κ=0.51), and semantically equivalent prompt templates change majority outcomes in 25% of cases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-as-a-Judge is a method where a large language model evaluates outputs from another LLM, often used to rank models or train reward models. Pairwise evaluation compares two outputs side-by-side, while pointwise evaluation assigns an absolute score. Position bias refers to the tendency to prefer the first option in a pair regardless of quality.

## [Editing One Neuron Can Fix LLM Repetition Loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13705) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers found that repetition loops in instruction-tuned LLMs like Gemma 4 can be traced to a small set of MLP neurons, and that suppressing these neurons with minimal weight edits—sometimes just a single sign-inverted neuron—can eliminate the loops while preserving general benchmark performance. This work provides a practical, interpretability-driven method for debugging specific failure modes in LLMs, showing that certain pathologies are localized and fixable without full retraining. It also highlights the limits of weight editing for knowledge-related issues like 'doom looping'. The study used per-layer ablation and per-neuron attribution to localize the cause, then confirmed candidates with full-generation sweeps. For the 26B-A4B Mixture-of-Experts model, the loops traced to a few routed experts rather than individual neurons.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Instruction-tuned LLMs are trained to follow user instructions, but they can exhibit repetitive generation failures, especially on long factual enumeration tasks. MLP (multilayer perceptron) layers in transformers are key components that process information; weight editing involves directly modifying model parameters to change behavior without retraining.

## [HierSVA: Pipeline, Dataset, Benchmark for LLM Hardware Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13706) ⭐️ 8.0/10

HierSVA introduces a complete pipeline, dataset, and benchmark for evaluating LLMs on hierarchical hardware formal verification, revealing that current LLMs achieve 67.1% module-level compile rate and 82.1% non-vacuous proof rate but only 70.2% fault detection and 36.2% formal core coverage. This work provides a standardized evaluation framework for LLM-driven hardware verification, a critical step toward automating formal verification in chip design, which could significantly reduce manual effort and improve design reliability. The HierSVA-DS dataset contains 342 modules from BaseJump STL with hierarchy metadata, and a deep subset of 28 module-bug pairs with natural-language specs. The benchmark defines six metric axes: syntax correctness, proof success rate, vacuity, specification faithfulness, mutation coverage, and formal core coverage.

## [Weight Norm Causal Law for Grokking Delay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13753) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper establishes a causal relationship between weight norm and grokking delay in neural networks, showing that when the norm is clamped, the delay follows an exponential law T_grok ∝ exp(α ρ) with α ≈ 7.5 across four modular arithmetic tasks. This provides a causal explanation for grokking, a phenomenon where neural networks suddenly generalize long after memorizing training data, which has puzzled researchers since its discovery in 2022. The findings could guide training strategies to control generalization timing. Under free training with weight decay, grokking occurs when weight norm reaches a critical value Wc that varies little across seeds and learning rates (CV 1-2%) and grows as a power law with modular base. Clamping the norm to a multiple ρ of Wc yields an exponential delay law with R² = 0.996, and the norm affects delay ~19x more than learning rate.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Grokking is a phenomenon in neural networks where the model first memorizes training data and then, after a long delay, suddenly achieves strong generalization. The role of weight norm in this transition has been debated: some studies suggest a critical norm triggers grokking, while others observe grokking without a fixed norm. This paper intervenes on the norm during training to settle the debate.

## [SuperThoughts Doubles LLM Reasoning Throughput](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13862) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SuperThoughts compresses pairs of consecutive Chain-of-Thought tokens into single latent representations and decodes two tokens per step via a lightweight Multi-Token Prediction module, achieving ~20-30% CoT length reduction and 2x inference throughput with minimal accuracy loss. This work addresses the high computational cost of long Chain-of-Thought reasoning, a major bottleneck for deploying LLMs in real-time applications. By doubling throughput without significant accuracy degradation, SuperThoughts could enable faster and more cost-effective reasoning in large language models. The method finetunes Qwen2.5-Math models (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and evaluates on MATH500, AMC, OlympiadBench, and GPQA-Diamond. A confidence-based adaptive mechanism falls back to standard decoding when uncertain, maintaining accuracy with only 1-2 points drop on most tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning improves LLM problem-solving by generating intermediate steps, but it is computationally expensive due to sequential token generation. Recent works explore reasoning in continuous latent spaces to bypass discrete tokens, but often suffer from training instability and poor scalability. SuperThoughts combines the benefits of discrete token supervision with latent space efficiency by compressing token pairs.

## [Mirage Probes Reveal Two Types of VLM Deception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13870) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Mirage Probes, a contrastive probing framework that shows vision-language models exhibit two distinct mirage behaviors—textual biases and visual hallucination—which can be linearly decoded from internal activations. This work challenges the assumption that mirage behavior is a single failure mode, and provides a framework to distinguish between language-prior-based and visual-representation-based errors, which has direct implications for VLM evaluation and safety. The framework uses paraphrased question variants paired with mirage/non-mirage labels on the same image, and introduces the Prior Harnessing Index (PHI) to measure how much a model answers from text alone. The two regimes require different mitigation strategies: text-distribution cleaning for textual biases, and representational-level interventions for spurious images.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) can answer image-based questions even when no image is provided, a phenomenon called mirage behavior that inflates benchmark scores. Prior work treated this as a single failure mode, but this paper shows it consists of two distinct types: textual biases (relying on language priors) and visual hallucination (constructing false visual content). The Mirage Probes framework uses linear decoding from internal activations to separate these behaviors.

## [Gefen: Memory-Efficient Optimizer Cuts AdamW Memory by 8x](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13894) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Gefen, a memory-efficient optimizer that reduces AdamW's memory footprint by approximately 8x by sharing second-moment estimates across parameter blocks and quantizing the first moment using a learned codebook. This addresses a critical memory bottleneck in large-scale deep learning, enabling training of larger models or larger batch sizes on the same hardware, and improving throughput in distributed training setups like FSDP and DDP. Gefen achieves a memory reduction of 6.5 GiB per billion parameters compared to AdamW, and it requires no architecture-specific metadata or hyperparameters beyond AdamW defaults, making it a practical drop-in replacement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: AdamW is a widely used optimizer in deep learning that maintains two moment estimates (first and second) for each parameter, effectively doubling the memory required for model states. Gefen reduces this overhead by sharing second-moment estimates among parameters with similar Hessian structure and quantizing the first moment via a learned codebook, inspired by theoretical insights about Hessian-aligned parameters.

## [HiLo-Token: Adaptive Token Compression for Faster Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13898) ⭐️ 8.0/10

HiLo-Token proposes an input-adaptive token compression framework for Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) that reduces latency in image editing by allocating more tokens to high-frequency regions and fewer to low-frequency areas, achieving up to 3.13x speedup on A100-80GB without quality regression. This addresses a critical latency bottleneck in DiT-based image editing tools like Photoshop, where the DiT module accounts for 73% of total latency even after distillation. The method is practical for production use, offering significant speedups across various mask ratios without compromising generation quality. The framework retains all tokens within a dilated mask for the editing region, uses high-frequency token selection outside the editing region, and represents low-frequency components with tokens from a 16x downsampled image. Experiments show 3.13x, 2.59x, and 1.67x DiT speedups for small, medium, and large mask ratios (average 6.38%, 15.92%, 35.36%) on A100-80GB.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) are a class of diffusion models that replace convolutional U-Nets with transformer architectures, offering scalability but often higher latency. Token compression techniques reduce computational cost by merging or pruning tokens, but existing methods are not input-adaptive. HiLo-Token leverages spatial frequency to adaptively allocate token budgets, preserving detail in high-frequency regions while compressing low-frequency areas.

STREAM introduces a three-tier routing architecture for LLM inference that unifies local, HPC, and cloud resources, featuring a dual-channel HPC streaming mechanism that achieves sub-second time-to-first-token (TTFT) through institutional firewalls without VPN modifications. ## [DeCompBench: Benchmarking LLM Agent Safety Against Decomposition Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13994) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced DeCompBench, a benchmark specifically designed to evaluate LLM agent safety against decomposition attacks that break harmful tasks into benign subtasks. Experiments show that state-of-the-art agents have significantly lower refusal rates on decomposed variants compared to monolithic harmful tasks. Decomposition attacks represent a novel and practical threat to deployed LLM agents, as they can bypass safety mechanisms by distributing malicious intent across multiple benign-looking steps. This benchmark provides a principled way to measure and improve agent safety against such attacks, which is critical for real-world deployment. DeCompBench is built using a decomposition-by-design principle with a graphical framework, enabling harmful task decomposition into individually benign and executable subtasks with realistic workflows. The dataset is publicly available on Hugging Face.

**Background**: LLM agents are increasingly capable and deployed in real-world applications, creating incentives for adversarial misuse. Decomposition attacks exploit the fact that safety alignment often evaluates each prompt in isolation, so breaking a harmful request into benign subtasks can evade detection. Prior benchmarks did not explicitly capture this form of misuse.

## [Safety Envelopes for VLA Driving Planners Under ISO 21448](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14238) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper evaluates the safety of Alpamayo R1, a 10B-parameter open-weight VLA driving planner, by analyzing when and how severely it fails under perturbations across 15,968 (clip, attack) pairs, revealing a gap between aggregate safe thresholds and scenario-specific severity bands. This work directly addresses safety certification of VLA driving planners under ISO 21448 (SOTIF), a critical step for deploying autonomous driving systems. The proposed two-dimensional safety envelope (combining aggregate thresholds and severity bands) could become a standard methodology for ODD specification. The study uses a Gaussian Mixture Model (GMM) on the changed-explanation subset to identify six discrete severity bands (BIC-optimal k=6). It finds that STOP_SIGNAL concentrates roughly 4× the high-severity (C4/C5) share of LANE_KEEPING despite tolerating a larger noise threshold σ.

## [Squeeze-Release: Iterative Pruning with Exact Structural Minimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14346) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Squeeze-Release introduces an iterative cycle that prunes a neural network, then exactly rewrites the masked sparse tensors into smaller dense ones (structural minimization), and finally releases the zeroed positions as small noise to recover trainable capacity. It also proposes CompensatedLayerNorm to extend minimization to LayerNorm-equipped architectures. This work addresses a key limitation of unstructured pruning: the deployed model remains the same size as the original. By achieving exact structural minimization, Squeeze-Release enables truly smaller deployable models, achieving up to 39x compression on fully-connected networks and 14.8x on ConvNeXt-Tiny, which can significantly reduce memory and compute costs in production. The Squeeze-Release cycle consists of three steps: squeeze (prune and minimize), release (add noise to zeroed positions), and retrain. CompensatedLayerNorm replaces standard LayerNorm with a function-preserving version that reconstructs full-width normalization from statistics of removed channels, enabling channel reduction in residual streams.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Unstructured pruning sets individual weights to zero, creating sparse tensors, but standard implementations keep the original tensor shape, so the model does not shrink in practice. Structural minimization rewrites a masked network into a smaller dense network with the same forward function, but prior methods could not handle LayerNorm or achieve iterative refinement. Squeeze-Release combines pruning, exact minimization, and a release step to iteratively discover and exploit structural redundancy.

Researchers propose Elastic Queries Reinforcement Learning (EQRL), a framework that adaptively selects latent input, denoising budget, and action chunk length for VLA models based on state difficulty, without fine-tuning the underlying model. EQRL addresses the rigidity of fixed inference schedules in VLA models, reducing computational cost while maintaining or improving task success in robot manipulation, which is crucial for deploying efficient and adaptive robotic systems. EQRL uses a lightweight latent-schedule adaptor to jointly select the latent input, denoising budget, and action chunk length, and trains a critic over the joint latent-schedule action to derive a state difficulty signal from critic ensemble disagreement.

EQRL introduces adaptive inference to allocate more computation to difficult states and less to easy ones.

This paper proposes a geometric theory of discovery bottlenecks, showing that non-local exploration (e.g., via LLM proposals) only helps under three specific conditions: spectral compression, orthogonal escape, and residual signal alignment. This framework provides a diagnostic tool for deciding when to use LLM-guided discovery, potentially improving efficiency in AI-driven scientific research across domains like finance and symbolic regression. The theory is validated through controlled synthetic experiments, large-scale A-share factor discovery, and symbolic regression benchmarks, showing that random orthogonal jumps expand coverage but do not improve yield without predictive alignment.

CADET introduces a training-free framework that audits, benchmarks, and repairs spurious correlations in pretrained end-to-end driving planners without any parameter update. This addresses a critical safety issue in autonomous driving—causal confusion—where planners rely on spurious cues rather than true causal factors, and it does so without costly retraining, making it practical for deployed systems. CADET uses causal graph analysis and intervention-based auditing to detect spurious correlations, then applies a training-free repair mechanism that adjusts the planner's outputs without modifying model weights.

This causal confusion is hard to detect because standard open-loop metrics like L2 displacement and collision rate are dominated by ego status and do not reveal reliance on spurious cues. Existing solutions require retraining large models, which is impractical for already-deployed systems.

HamEvo, a fixed-point neural operator, learns the single-step self-consistent update for Hamiltonian prediction, achieving 35-49% error reduction over baselines on benchmarks from MD17 to QMugs. This work significantly accelerates density functional theory (DFT) calculations while preserving access to molecular orbitals and energy levels, with potential impact on computational chemistry and materials science. Machine learning models that directly predict the converged Hamiltonian can accelerate DFT, but previous approaches often fail to capture the fixed-point nature of the SCF iteration. HamEvo addresses this by learning the single-step update and returning the converged Hamiltonian as its fixed point.

## [AI Agents Challenge Open Source Governance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14594) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper analyzes how autonomous AI contributors strain open-source governance and maps fragmented policy responses to emerging AI regulations like the EU AI Act and NIST AI RMF. This matters because AI agents are increasingly submitting pull requests with limited human oversight, creating operational risks that current open-source policies are not designed to handle, and the lack of harmonized governance could undermine trust in open-source software. The paper compares policies across six organizations (SymPy, LLVM, matplotlib, OpenInfra, Apache Software Foundation, Linux Foundation) and derives a six-dimensional taxonomy and a Policy Maturity Score, mapping documented agent incidents onto governance gaps.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Open-source software development relies on human-centric processes like contributor agreements and code review, which assume a legally accountable person. Autonomous AI agents that can plan changes and submit pull requests break these assumptions, leading to incidents such as AI-generated nuisance volume and platform shutdowns. Emerging AI regulations like the EU AI Act and NIST AI RMF provide high-level frameworks but have not been mapped to contribution-level governance.

## [Verifier-Driven Self-DPO Can Cause VLM Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14629) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study shows that verifier-driven self-DPO, a common self-improvement method for VLMs, can silently regress student performance when verifier quality is task-specific, contradicting the assumption of monotonic improvement. This finding challenges a widely held assumption in VLM self-improvement, warning practitioners that using a strong verifier does not guarantee better student performance across all tasks, and could even cause significant degradation. On MathVista, MMMU, and BLINK, the same verifiers that improve a Qwen-3-VL-2B student on MathVista cause drops of 3.4 to 10.9 percentage points on MMMU, where verifier rubric accuracy falls to 8%-23%. The regression replicates on Qwen-2.5-VL-3B and is confidence-inverted: more accurate-but-still-wrong verifiers cause larger regression.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Verifier-driven self-DPO is a method where a frozen verifier scores candidate outputs from a VLM, and the top and bottom scores form preference pairs used to update the model via Direct Preference Optimization (DPO). The common assumption is that a stronger verifier leads to a stronger student, but this paper shows that verifier quality is task-specific, and when verifier accuracy on a target task is low, the student can regress.

## [Trivia Is Provably Necessary for Valuable Math Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14688) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new theoretical paper models the generation of valuable mathematics using proof assistants, proving that verifiers cannot replace human taste but can ensure sound coverage, and that an infinite but asymptotically negligible stream of certified trivia is necessary to cover unrecorded valuable mathematics. This work addresses a key bottleneck in AI-assisted formal mathematics: the gap between what a proof checker can verify and what mathematicians value. It provides a theoretical foundation for understanding why AI systems inevitably produce correct-but-worthless statements, which has implications for AI-generated content and formal verification. The paper introduces a model of nested language generation in the limit, where a verifiable formal language F contains an unknown valuable language H revealed via an adversarial core C of density α. It shows a sharp dichotomy: generators emitting finitely many trivia achieve optimal coverage α/2, while any infinite trivia allowance jumps the optimum to 1-α/2.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Language generation in the limit is a theoretical framework where a learner must eventually output only valid new examples from an unknown target language. Proof assistants are software systems that formally certify mathematical results, but they cannot judge mathematical value. This paper combines these ideas to analyze the trade-off between correctness and value in AI-generated mathematics.

A controlled ablation study reveals that storing verbatim conversation chunks outperforms extracted structured artifacts for long LLM conversation memory, with accuracy gaps of 15.9 points on LoCoMo and 22.0 points on LongMemEval-S. This challenges the common assumption that distilled structured representations are superior for retrieval in conversational AI, suggesting that verbatim text should be retained rather than replaced by extracted artifacts. The study uses a fixed retrieval-rerank-reasoning pipeline, swapping only the stored representation, and finds that even a 1-hop semantic graph cannot recover the gap; a union store of chunks and artifacts matches chunks alone.

## [FusionRoute: Token-Level LLM Collaboration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.05106) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FusionRoute introduces a lightweight router that simultaneously selects the best expert model per token and adds a complementary logit to correct the expert's output, enabling token-level collaboration among specialized LLMs. This approach addresses the trade-off between general-purpose LLM scalability and specialized model generalization, potentially enabling more efficient and accurate multi-domain AI systems without massive model sizes. FusionRoute is theoretically shown to overcome limitations of pure expert-only routing, and empirically outperforms sequence-level collaboration, model merging, and fine-tuning across Llama-3 and Gemma-2 families on math, code, and instruction-following benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often require massive scale to perform well across diverse domains, while smaller specialized models are efficient but lack generalization. Token-level collaboration dynamically selects the best expert for each token, but prior methods only use fixed expert outputs, limiting optimality. FusionRoute augments this with a trainable complementary generator that refines predictions.

## [Interpretability Must Be Defined via Symmetries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.12913) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper argues that AI interpretability must be defined in terms of four symmetries—inference equivariance, information invariance, concept-closure invariance, and structural invariance—to enable formal testing and design. It proposes a unified framework that treats interpretable inference as Bayesian inversion and allows compliance verification. This work addresses a fundamental gap in current interpretability definitions, which are often vague and untestable. By providing formal, testable conditions, it could significantly advance AI safety, regulation, and trustworthiness. The paper hypothesizes that four symmetries suffice to formalize interpretable models as a subclass of probabilistic models. It unifies alignment, interventions, and counterfactuals under a Bayesian inversion framework.

**Background**: AI interpretability aims to make model decisions understandable to humans, helping debug, detect bias, and ensure compliance. Researchers introduced InfoReasoner, a framework that uses a synthetic semantic information gain reward to optimize retrieval in agentic reasoning, with theoretical guarantees and a practical estimator. It achieves up to 5.4% average accuracy improvement across seven QA benchmarks. This work provides a principled and scalable method for improving retrieval in agentic reasoning, addressing the lack of dense reward signals. It could enhance the performance of large reasoning models in knowledge-intensive tasks, impacting AI systems that require dynamic information seeking. The intrinsic reward is computed via an output-aware estimator using semantic clustering and bidirectional textual entailment, without manual annotations. Training uses Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), and the code is open-sourced on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic reasoning allows large reasoning models to dynamically acquire external knowledge during inference, but optimizing the retrieval process is challenging due to the lack of principled reward signals. Information gain measures the reduction in uncertainty, and InfoReasoner uses it as an intrinsic reward to guide the agent toward acquiring uncertainty-resolving information. This approach is theoretically grounded with guarantees like non-negativity and telescoping additivity.

Researchers propose ViewSuite, a benchmark for evaluating vision-language models (VLMs) on view planning, and an iterative self-exploration framework with view graph distillation that improves Qwen2.5-VL-7B from 2.5% to 47.8% on interactive view planning, surpassing GPT-5.4 Pro and Gemini 3.1 Pro. ViewSuite is built on ~300 real ScanNet indoor scenes with ~55K view pairs and ~165K task instances, probing view planning through three diagnostic tasks. This work addresses the critical challenge of trust calibration when using LLMs as heuristic advisors in optimization, which is increasingly relevant as LLMs are applied to scientific and engineering design tasks where different objectives may require different expertise. MiniMax introduces MiniMax Sparse Attention (MSA), a blockwise sparse attention mechanism built on Grouped Query Attention (GQA) that uses a lightweight Index Branch for Top-k block selection, achieving 28.4x reduction in attention compute at 1M context and 14.2x prefill speedup on H800 GPUs. The practical speedups and open-source release make long-context deployment more feasible. MSA co-designs an exp-free Top-k selection and KV-outer sparse attention kernel to improve tensor-core utilization under block-granular access. On a 109B multimodal model, MSA matches GQA quality while reducing per-token attention compute by 28.4x at 1M context, with 7.6x decoding speedup.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Standard softmax attention has quadratic complexity with sequence length, making it prohibitive for long contexts. Grouped Query Attention (GQA) reduces memory by sharing key/value heads across query groups. Blockwise sparse attention partitions sequences into blocks and selects only relevant blocks, balancing efficiency and quality.

DiffusionBlocks proposes a novel framework that converts residual connections in transformers into a denoising process, enabling independent block-wise training using score matching objectives. This approach significantly reduces memory requirements for training large transformers by allowing gradient computation on only one block at a time, potentially enabling training of larger models on limited hardware. The method requires minimal modifications to the residual network and works across vision, diffusion, autoregressive, recurrent-depth, and masked diffusion transformers, matching end-to-end performance.

Diffusion models learn to reverse a noise process via score matching, which estimates the gradient of the data distribution's log-density.

This work provides a principled, theoretically grounded approach to interpretability, helping researchers uncover biases, translation strategies, and in-context learning mechanisms in LLMs. The methods are grounded in perturbation theory and information geometry, and are demonstrated through case studies on instruction understanding, translation, and in-context time-series forecasting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Token-level attribution quantifies each input token's contribution to a model's output, often using attention analysis, gradient methods, or causal ablation. Jacobian Scopes extends this by leveraging gradients to capture causal influences on multiple aspects of the prediction, including logits, distribution, and uncertainty.

## [SMART: Mesh-Free Aerodynamic Surrogate Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18707) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced SMART, a transformer-based surrogate model that predicts aerodynamic quantities from raw point-cloud geometries without requiring simulation meshes. This approach eliminates the computationally costly mesh generation step, enabling faster aerodynamic simulations for engineering design optimization while maintaining accuracy comparable to mesh-based methods. SMART encodes geometry and simulation parameters into a shared latent space and uses a physics decoder with cross-layer attention to map spatial queries to physical quantities, outperforming existing mesh-free methods on industry-level benchmarks.

**Background**: Traditional aerodynamic simulations require generating a computational mesh around the geometry, which is time-consuming and expensive. Surrogate models using machine learning can accelerate simulations, but many still rely on the mesh as input. Mesh-free methods avoid mesh generation but typically suffer from higher errors. SMART addresses this gap by using a point cloud representation and a transformer architecture to achieve high accuracy without meshes.

ULEE uses an in-context learner that adapts across episodes and an adversarial goal generator that maintains training at the frontier of the agent's capabilities. The method generalizes to novel objectives, environment dynamics, and map structures.

## [V-pretraining: Learning What to Predict for Downstream Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22108) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose V-pretraining, a method that uses a lightweight task designer to dynamically select self-supervised targets for each batch, guided by a small set of downstream examples, without directly supervising the main model. This enables step-level feedback during continued pretraining, improving target capabilities like math reasoning and vision transfer. V-pretraining bridges the gap between self-supervised pretraining and downstream task performance, offering a more efficient and targeted adaptation than traditional coarse feedback loops. It could reduce the need for large labeled datasets and manual task design, benefiting both language and vision models. In language modeling, V-pretraining uses adaptive top-K soft targets; in vision, it learns views or masks. Experiments show a +7.4 point gain on GSM8K for Qwen2.5-0.5B using only 1,024 examples as feedback, and improved DINOv3 transfer to segmentation and depth estimation without sacrificing ImageNet accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Continued pretraining typically uses fixed self-supervised tasks (e.g., masked language modeling) selected based on downstream validation performance, creating a coarse feedback loop. V-pretraining introduces a task designer that predicts the downstream loss reduction from a candidate self-supervised update, allowing step-level adaptation without direct supervision on the learner.

## [Residual Context Diffusion Boosts LLM Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22954) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Residual Context Diffusion (RCD), a module that recycles discarded token representations in diffusion LLMs by converting them into contextual residuals and injecting them back into the denoising process. RCD uses a decoupled two-stage training pipeline to avoid memory bottlenecks and achieves 4-11 percentage point accuracy improvements with minimal extra computation. This work addresses a key inefficiency in diffusion LLMs, which waste computation by discarding low-confidence tokens during iterative denoising. By recycling these representations, RCD improves both efficiency and accuracy, potentially enabling faster and more capable LLM inference for long-chain reasoning and instruction following tasks. RCD is validated on both long CoT reasoning (SDAR) and short CoT instruction following (LLaDA) models, converting a standard dLLM to the RCD paradigm with only ~300 million tokens. On the challenging AIME tasks, RCD nearly doubles baseline accuracy and achieves 4-5x fewer denoising steps at the baseline's peak accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion Large Language Models (dLLMs) generate text by iteratively denoising a sequence of tokens, similar to image diffusion models. Unlike autoregressive models that generate one token at a time, dLLMs can decode multiple tokens in parallel, but they rely on a remasking mechanism that keeps only the most confident tokens and discards the rest, wasting computation. Residual Context Diffusion (RCD) reuses these discarded representations as contextual residuals to improve subsequent denoising steps.

## [FAST-AR: Training-Free Attention Framework for Video Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01801) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose FAST-AR, a training-free attention framework that reduces KV cache redundancy and latency in autoregressive video diffusion models, achieving up to 5-10x end-to-end speedups while maintaining visual quality. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in autoregressive video diffusion models, enabling efficient long-form video generation and world models with stable throughput and constant GPU memory usage, which is essential for interactive applications like neural game engines. FAST-AR consists of three components: TempCache compresses the KV cache via temporal correspondence, AnnCA accelerates cross-attention by selecting frame-relevant prompt tokens, and AnnSA sparsifies self-attention using approximate nearest neighbor matching.

**Background**: Autoregressive video diffusion models generate videos frame by frame, but the KV cache grows linearly with video length, causing increasing latency and memory usage. Training-free caching methods reuse intermediate computations when changes are small, but prior approaches still suffer from progressive slowdown and memory growth. FAST-AR introduces a unified framework that combines temporal cache compression and sparse attention to overcome these limitations.

## [QES Enables High-Precision Fine-Tuning of Quantized LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03120) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Quantized Evolution Strategies (QES), a new optimization paradigm that performs full-parameter fine-tuning directly in the quantized space without backpropagation, using accumulated error feedback and stateless seed replay. QES overcomes the fundamental challenge of fine-tuning quantized LLMs, which are otherwise static after post-training quantization, enabling efficient adaptation on memory-constrained devices without expensive retraining pipelines. QES integrates accumulated error feedback to preserve high-precision weight updating signals and uses stateless seed replay to reduce memory usage to low-precision inference levels, significantly outperforming state-of-the-art zeroth-order fine-tuning methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Post-training quantization (PTQ) reduces model size and memory footprint by lowering the precision of weights and activations, but it makes models difficult to fine-tune because standard methods like backpropagation require continuous, differentiable parameters. Evolution strategies (ES) are derivative-free optimization algorithms that can handle discrete spaces, but they often suffer from vanishing or inaccurate gradient estimates when applied to quantized parameters. QES addresses these issues with two key innovations: error feedback and stateless seed replay.

## [DPPO Fixes PPO's Flaw for LLM Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.04879) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper argues that PPO's ratio clipping is ill-suited for LLMs due to noisy Monte Carlo estimates, and proposes DPPO, which replaces clipping with a direct policy divergence constraint using efficient Binary and Top-K approximations. This could significantly improve the stability and efficiency of RL-based fine-tuning for LLMs, addressing a fundamental flaw in the widely-used PPO algorithm and potentially leading to better-performing language models. DPPO uses Total Variation or KL divergence as a constraint instead of ratio clipping, and introduces Binary and Top-K approximations to avoid large memory overhead. The paper provides code at https://github.com/sail-sg/Stable-RL.

**Background**: PPO is a popular reinforcement learning algorithm that uses ratio clipping to keep policy updates within a trust region. However, for LLMs with large vocabularies, the ratio is a noisy single-sample Monte Carlo estimate, leading to suboptimal updates. DPPO addresses this by directly constraining policy divergence.

## [Metabolic cost emerges naturally in Poisson VAEs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13421) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper shows that Poisson variational autoencoders (P-VAEs) inherently include a metabolic cost term that couples coding rate to firing rate, enabling a trade-off between coding fidelity and energy expenditure. This coupling is absent in standard Gaussian VAEs. This work bridges variational free energy minimization and biological energy constraints, offering a principled framework for energy-aware computation. It has potential impact on understanding biological neural computation and designing more energy-efficient AI models. The KL divergence term in the Poisson free energy objective becomes proportional to prior firing rates, creating an emergent metabolic cost that penalizes high baseline activity. Experiments show that increasing the KL weight β monotonically increases sparsity and reduces spiking activity in P-VAE, but not in a Gaussian VAE with ReLU rectification (Grelu-VAE).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Variational autoencoders (VAEs) are generative models that learn latent representations by minimizing a variational free energy objective. Standard VAEs assume Gaussian latent variables, which do not account for the energy cost of neural activity. The Poisson VAE encodes inputs as discrete spike counts, making it more biologically realistic and allowing a natural link between information-theoretic coding rate and biophysical firing rate.

FedRot-LoRA proposes a novel method to mitigate rotational misalignment in federated LoRA by aligning client updates via orthogonal transformations before aggregation, without increasing communication cost. This addresses a fundamental issue in federated LoRA where factor-wise averaging causes destructive interference due to rotational invariance, improving the stability and accuracy of federated fine-tuning for large language models. The method provides a convergence analysis showing that rotational alignment yields a tighter upper bound on aggregation error, and experiments on NLU and generative tasks show consistent improvements over existing baselines across various heterogeneity levels and LoRA ranks.

This work bridges the gap between supervised fine-tuning and task performance in 3D perception, enabling smaller models (e.g., 4B parameters) to outperform larger ones on key benchmarks, which could accelerate progress in autonomous driving, robotics, and AR/VR. 3D-RFT first activates a 3D-aware Multi-modal Large Language Model (MLLM) via SFT, then applies GRPO with reward functions derived directly from 3D IoU and F1-Score. ## [Mean Bias Causes FP4 Quantization Failure in LLM Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10444) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers discovered that a coherent rank-one mean bias, not arbitrary outliers, is the primary cause of FP4 quantization failure in LLM training, and proposed Averis, a simple mean-residual splitting method that isolates the mean before quantization. This insight overturns the common assumption that activation outliers are random, enabling a simpler and more hardware-efficient solution for low-bit LLM training, which is crucial for reducing memory and compute costs. Averis uses only reductions and elementwise subtractions before FP4 quantization, achieving 1.19%/0.81% BF16 loss gaps on Qwen3 models, outperforming NVIDIA's Hadamard-based method (2.05%/1.10%), with only 2.20% end-to-end overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: FP4 (4-bit floating point) training promises significant memory and compute savings for large language models (LLMs), but blockwise quantization is often disrupted by extreme activation magnitudes, which inflate dynamic range and compress long-tail signals. Previous approaches like Hadamard-based outlier smoothing are complex and hardware-intensive. This paper identifies that the dominant outliers are not random but stem from a coherent rank-one mean bias that aligns with the leading anisotropic spectral component.

A new study reveals that large language models (LLMs) exhibit up to 50% drops in compliance with formatting instructions when simultaneously performing demanding tasks, and proposes a salience-enhanced format that restores compliance to 90-100%. This work highlights a critical reliability issue in LLMs—prospective memory failures—that can undermine their use in applications requiring strict output formatting, such as data extraction or code generation. The study tested three model families across over 8,000 prompts, finding that terminal constraints (e.g., ending with a specific token) degrade most severely, while avoidance constraints are more robust; interference is bidirectional, with formatting constraints also reducing task accuracy by up to 66 percentage points.

## [LLM Lifespan in Science Shrinks Rapidly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07530) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces time-to-peak and lifespan metrics for LLM obsolescence, analyzing 62 models across 108k citing papers from 2019-2025, and finds that each successive release year is associated with a 27% shorter time-to-peak and a 23% shorter lifespan. This reveals that specialization on any single LLM is a depreciating investment, with reproducibility and migration costs increasing, which has significant implications for AI research and software engineering practices. The study uses an inverted-U curve to model LLM adoption, separating active adoption from background citation, and finds that release year predicts obsolescence more strongly than architecture, openness, or scale.

**Background**: Scaling laws describe how LLM capabilities grow with compute and data, but they ignore how long a model remains relevant after release. This paper addresses that gap by introducing metrics for model obsolescence, analyzing citation patterns to measure the lifespan of scientific impact.

## [LLM Jury Shows Promise for Scoring Medical Diagnoses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14892) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study evaluates an LLM Jury composed of three frontier AI models for scoring 3334 diagnoses across 300 real-world hospital cases from low- and middle-income countries (LMICs). The uncalibrated LLM Jury systematically underestimates scores but preserves ordinal agreement with expert panels, and calibration via isotonic regression yields excellent agreement. This research addresses a critical bottleneck in medical AI evaluation—the high cost and slow pace of expert clinician panels—by demonstrating that a calibrated LLM Jury can serve as a trustworthy proxy. If validated in other settings, it could accelerate the deployment of AI diagnostic tools, especially in resource-limited environments. The LLM Jury scored diagnoses across four dimensions: diagnosis, differential diagnosis, clinical reasoning, and negative treatment risk. The study found no self-preference bias, meaning the LLM Jury did not favor diagnoses from its own underlying models. Post hoc calibration using isotonic regression significantly improved score accuracy.
