## [EmoMind Decodes Affective Captions from fMRI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16739) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EmoMind is the first end-to-end pipeline that decodes affective captions directly from fMRI signals by combining a semantically grounded scene description with a continuous 34-dimensional emotion vector. It uses classifier-free guidance to smoothly control the balance between content preservation and affective expression. This work addresses a key gap in brain-to-text systems, which typically discard affect, and demonstrates that continuous brain-decoded affect can serve as a viable control signal for individualized affective caption generation. It outperforms label-prompted GPT-4 on subject-specific affective structure, opening new directions for affective computing and neuroscience. EmoMind uses a two-stage approach: first retrieving a neutral scene description from brain-decoded visual features, then rewriting it using a 34-dimensional emotion vector decoded from the same fMRI recording. The rewriter is trained with classifier-free guidance against an identity-preserving null branch, enabling smooth interpolation between semantic fidelity and affective expressivity.

**Background**: Functional magnetic resonance imaging (fMRI) measures brain activity by detecting changes in blood flow, and brain decoding aims to reconstruct perceptual experiences from these signals. ## [Exact Linear Attention Achieves Linear Complexity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18848) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Exact Linear Attention (ELA) is a new mechanism that achieves linear computational complexity for Transformer attention via exact kernel decomposition, eliminating approximation errors. It also introduces Hyper-Link, Memory Lobe, and a routing-score-based MoE bias to address gradient explosion and token dilution. ELA enables Transformers to scale to ultra-long sequences with up to 6x faster decoding and 75% less KV cache memory, making it highly impactful for large language models and vision tasks. Its theoretical guarantees and practical innovations could accelerate deployment of efficient AI systems. The paper proposes several kernel functions like Hadamard Exp Kernel and Summation Squared Euclidean Distance Kernel, each tailored for specific attention behaviors. Experiments show ELA achieves up to 4.3x GPU inference speedup and 7.9x parameter reduction in vision models (YOLO-LAT) with competitive accuracy.

**Background**: Standard Transformer attention has quadratic complexity O(L²) in sequence length L, which becomes prohibitive for long sequences. ELA overcomes these by enforcing kernel constraints for non-negativity, discriminability, and geometric interpretability.

## [Third-Order SO(3) Invariant for Robust Spherical Watermarking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26702) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a principled third-order invariant construction for spherical watermarking by coupling higher-order SO(3) irreducible representations via tensor products, enabling rotation-invariant descriptors with higher embedding capacity. This work addresses the fundamental challenge of arbitrary 3D rotations in panoramic imagery watermarking with provable guarantees, potentially impacting 3D vision and multimedia security by enabling robust watermarking without sacrificing capacity. The method uses spherical harmonic coefficients that transform equivariantly under rotations, but constructs a spherical invariant bispectrum that preserves phase information while remaining strictly rotation-invariant. Watermarks are embedded into higher-order coefficients and recovered from invariant bispectral scalars.

This work extends to third-order invariants via tensor products of irreducible representations.

Researchers propose Patcher, a post-hoc defense framework that repairs backdoored large language models using only a single reported failure case and model parameters, without requiring attack knowledge or multiple triggered examples. This work addresses a critical practical gap in LLM security, as defenders often observe only one failure case and cannot distinguish backdoor attacks from natural alignment bugs. Patcher enables practical, low-cost defense for deployed models. ## [LLM Hallucination Detection as Quickest Change Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12476) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper formulates hallucination onset detection in LLMs as a quickest change detection problem, deriving Lorden's lower bound (~1.3 tokens at false-alarm rate 0.01) and showing that a causal recurrent labeler (CUSUM-like) detects onsets in 11-13 tokens, outperforming linear baselines (31 tokens). This work provides a principled framework for evaluating and improving real-time hallucination monitors, addressing a critical reliability issue in LLM deployment. The theoretical bounds and empirical insights could guide the development of faster, more accurate detection systems. At a false-alarm budget of 0.01, every detector catches under a third of onsets, and the recall-honest delay is 56-66 tokens, indicating low-false-alarm onset detection is hard. The speed advantage of the recurrent labeler is attributed mostly to better per-token scoring rather than temporal accumulation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Quickest change detection (QCD) is a statistical framework for detecting abrupt changes in data streams as quickly as possible after they occur, with a constraint on false alarms. The CUSUM algorithm is a classic QCD method that accumulates log-likelihood ratios. Lorden's lower bound provides a theoretical minimum detection delay for a given false-alarm rate. This paper applies these concepts to token-level hallucination detection in LLMs, where the goal is to raise an alarm as soon as a model begins generating unfaithful content.

MAStrike introduces a closed-loop framework for collusive red-teaming in hierarchical multi-agent systems, using Shapley values to quantify each agent's contribution to system robustness and identify vulnerable coalitions for coordinated attacks. This work addresses a critical gap in multi-agent system safety by enabling systematic identification of the most influential agents and their collusive vulnerabilities, which is essential for high-stakes domains like finance and software engineering where coordinated adversarial behaviors can bypass existing defenses. MAStrike includes the first agent-level Shapley value analysis for MAS, a comprehensive red-teaming benchmark with controllable environments across finance, software engineering, and CRM, and experiments on multiple frontier models showing substantial improvement over heuristic baselines.

The Shapley value is a game-theoretic concept that fairly distributes credit among players based on their marginal contributions to a coalition's outcome.

## [Order Is Not Control: New Response Law for AI and Biology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12923) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper argues that order does not imply control and proposes a receiver-gated response law, validated across biological systems (mouse ALM, C. elegans, zebrafish) and artificial neural networks (LLMs, adapters, stochastic operators). This framework provides a formal distinction between order and control, with potential implications for AI alignment, interpretability, and steering, by offering a measurable criterion for when an intervention truly controls a system. The law uses a denominator-indexed operator mapping material state, drive, bath, and receiver state to response displacement and sinks. LLM experiments achieved 72.8-73.7% component-sign accuracy for response vectors, rising to 84.3-84.8% on nonzero components.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Control theory traditionally focuses on steering systems toward desired states, but distinguishing mere order (e.g., patterns) from genuine control has been challenging. This work introduces a driven-dissipative response framework, treating systems as open to drives and dissipation, and defines control as finite effort moving a target while keeping unwanted effects bounded.

## [TWLA: Ternary Weights and Low-Bit Activations for LLMs via PTQ](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13054) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TWLA proposes a post-training quantization framework that achieves 1.58-bit ternary weights and 4-bit activations for large language models, overcoming the challenge of heavy-tailed activation distributions. This enables significant inference acceleration and memory reduction for LLMs, making deployment more practical on resource-constrained devices. It addresses a key bottleneck in LLM quantization by enabling low-bit activations alongside ternary weights. The framework includes three components: E2M-ATQ for layer-output error minimization, KOTMS for reshaping weights and suppressing activation outliers via Kronecker-structured orthogonal rotation, and ILA-AMP for mixed-precision activation allocation considering inter-layer interactions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) require significant memory and compute, hindering deployment. Quantization reduces precision of weights and activations to lower bit-widths, but existing methods often keep activations in high precision due to heavy-tailed distributions, limiting acceleration. Ternarization compresses weights to three values (-1, 0, +1), achieving 1.58-bit representation.

A new paper compares difference-in-means (DiM) and Iterative Nullspace Projection (INLP) interventions for steering refusal in safety-tuned chat models, finding that INLP counterfactual flipping is competitive with DiM directional ablation. This research provides practical insights into alternative refusal steering methods, which is crucial for AI safety and alignment. Understanding the geometric differences between interventions could lead to more controllable and interpretable models. The study tested five open-weight chat models, showing that nullspace projection is consistently weaker than counterfactual flipping. Restricting INLP to leading directions preserves suppression effects with near-baseline perplexity, offering a tunable capability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Previous work by Arditi et al. (2024) showed that refusal in safety-tuned LLMs is mediated by a single linear direction in the residual stream, recoverable via difference-in-means (DiM). INLP is a method for removing information from neural representations by iteratively training linear classifiers and projecting onto their nullspace. This paper extends INLP to counterfactual flipping, which moves activations into the opposite cluster rather than collapsing them between clusters.

## [AI Literacy-Usage Link Varies by Tool Type](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13734) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A reanalysis of Tully et al. (2025) Study 3 data finds that the negative association between AI literacy and usage is driven entirely by non-text AI tools, with no significant link for text AI tools. This finding refines the understanding of AI adoption, showing that lower literacy predicts broader adoption of less-penetrated tools rather than general AI receptivity, which has implications for AI education and product design. Using ordered-logit models with demographic adjustment, AI literacy did not significantly predict text AI usage (β = -0.090, p = .387) but strongly predicted non-text AI adoption (β = -0.377, p < .001). The non-text effect is primarily an adoption/non-adoption pattern, with an odds ratio of 0.68 for ever having used a non-text AI tool.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Ordinary least squares (OLS) regression is a linear method to model the relationship between a dependent variable and one or more independent variables. Ordered logit models are used for ordinal outcomes, while multinomial logit models handle categorical outcomes with more than two categories. These methods help analyze how AI literacy relates to usage frequency across different tool types.

## [HDC Enables Interpretable Similarity for Tabular Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13871) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes using Hyperdimensional Computing (HDC), specifically Holographic Reduced Representations (HRR), to enable interpretable similarity scores and zero-match detection in tabular data embeddings, addressing a key limitation of existing methods. This work could significantly improve data integration and profiling pipelines by allowing practitioners to set principled thresholds for retrieval and reliably detect when no valid match exists, which is crucial for real-world deployment. The authors derive closed-form expected similarity values for equality and non-equality predicates, which converge to interpretable values as dimensionality increases, and evaluate HDC against EmbDI on two real-world datasets, showing HDC matches or outperforms EmbDI while uniquely enabling zero-match detection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Tabular data embeddings convert rows, columns, or tables into vectors for tasks like entity resolution and schema matching. However, existing embeddings lack interpretable similarity scores, meaning the distance to the nearest neighbor does not indicate whether it is a true match. Hyperdimensional Computing (HDC) represents information as high-dimensional vectors (hypervectors) and uses algebraic operations like circular convolution to encode structure, enabling principled similarity computation.

Researchers introduced FactoryLLM, an open-source AI playground for evaluating LLM-based RAG models in smart factory fault diagnostics, with a focus on safety and local execution. FactoryLLM addresses the challenge of dispersed critical information across multiple machine manuals in smart factories, enabling safe evaluation of LLMs without exposing sensitive industrial data, which is crucial for industrial adoption of AI. FactoryLLM supports a dual evaluation setup using both RAGAS and NVIDIA's LLM-as-a-Judge metrics, and in a case study with an Autonomous Intelligent Vehicle, all tested LLMs achieved a groundedness score above 0.88 across 30 maintenance queries from 600 pages of documentation.

**Background**: RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation) combines document retrieval with LLM generation to answer queries based on specific documents. RAGAS is a framework for evaluating RAG pipelines using metrics like faithfulness and answer relevancy, while LLM-as-a-Judge uses another LLM to score outputs based on a rubric. FactoryLLM provides a controlled environment to test these methods on industrial documentation.

## [VeriGeo: Controllable Geometry Question Generation with Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14176) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced VeriGeo, a framework for generating geometry problems with executable reasoning traces that ensure consistency between the problem, diagram, constraints, and solution through numerical and analytical verification. This work addresses a key challenge in AI-assisted education and multimodal reasoning by enabling reliable, controllable generation of geometry problems, which can improve the quality of synthetic training data for multimodal LLMs. VeriGeo uses a three-stage pipeline that checks numerical consistency, analytical realizability, and global consistency, with verification-guided reflection to repair or reject invalid generations. Supervised fine-tuning on 8.7k VeriGeo-generated examples achieved the best reported GeoQA performance among end-to-end multimodal LLM-based solvers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Geometry problem generation is challenging because the problem statement, diagram, constraints, and solution must be mutually consistent. Existing methods either lack controllability or reliability: seed-based rewriting is flexible but weakly verifiable, while diagram-first construction improves validity but is less suited to arbitrary user constraints. VeriGeo introduces executable reasoning traces that connect natural language, diagrams, geometric constraints, and proof steps into a verifiable representation.

## [Affordance20Q: Benchmarking LLM Affordance Reasoning via 20 Questions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14240) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Affordance20Q, a benchmark that tests LLMs' affordance reasoning from physical properties using a 20-Questions game without revealing object identity, and developed KARI, a pipeline that improves open-source LLMs by up to 15.2 points. This benchmark addresses a key limitation in existing affordance evaluations, which often leak object identity and allow models to rely on memorized mappings rather than genuine physical reasoning, thus providing a more rigorous test of LLMs' physical understanding. Affordance20Q comprises 1,009 games over 454 objects and 59 affordances, all manually filtered and annotated, and experiments with 15 state-of-the-art LLMs show a ~20 point gap compared to human performance, with KL-based information-gain analysis revealing models fail to ask discriminating questions as the game progresses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Affordance reasoning is the ability to infer what actions an object enables based on its physical properties like shape and material. Existing benchmarks often expose the object's identity, allowing LLMs to use memorized knowledge instead of reasoning from properties. Affordance20Q hides the object identity, forcing models to ask yes/no questions about physical properties to identify affordances.

## [Self-Evolving Communication Policies for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14314) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper formalizes Communication Policy for LLM agents, comparing text-based and UI-based interactions, and proposes Communication Policy Evolution (CPE), a self-evolution framework that refines policies through rollout and prompt-level evolution without model modification. This work addresses the critical yet underexplored dimension of communication behavior in LLM agents, offering a hybrid method that combines the complementary strengths of text and UI interactions to improve task performance and persona compliance. The paper sets up two complementary settings: User-Agent and Planner-Executor, and finds that text-based interaction often facilitates task performance, while structured UI improves response quality and persona compliance. CPE achieves the best task success across multiple settings using only prompt refinement.

## [CSPO: Constraint-Sensitive Policy Optimization for Safe RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14415) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CSPO introduces a constraint-sensitive correction to primal-dual methods for safe reinforcement learning, enabling faster safety recovery and reducing oscillations near the safety boundary. This addresses a key limitation of primal-dual methods—delayed constraint correction—which often leads to prolonged safety violations in real-world applications like robotics and autonomous driving. CSPO augments the primal objective with a correction derived from the shortest signed distance to the safety boundary, preserving KKT solutions of the original constrained problem.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Safe reinforcement learning aims to maximize reward while satisfying safety constraints, often modeled as Constrained Markov Decision Processes (CMDPs). Primal-dual methods are popular for solving CMDPs but suffer from delayed Lagrange multiplier updates, causing oscillations and slow safety recovery.

## [Dense Coordinate-List Fine-Tuning Induces Repeat Behavior in VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14507) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study reveals that fine-tuning vision-language models (VLMs) like Gemma 4 12B with dense coordinate lists causes a controllable interference surface, leading to repeated outputs. The researchers propose an object-level repeat-stop method that eliminates duplicates without harming grounding accuracy. This finding is significant because it identifies a previously unknown side effect of fine-tuning VLMs for structured output generation, which is crucial for applications like visual grounding and object detection. The proposed mitigation technique enables reliable use of dense coordinate outputs without sacrificing performance. In Gemma 4 12B, high-capacity q/k/v/o LoRA raised class-aware F1@0.3 from 0.007 to 0.448 but induced a duplicate rate of 0.080 and max repeat of 23. The object-level repeat-stop reduced duplicate rate to 0.000 and max repeat to 1 while preserving F1@0.3 (0.494 to 0.490) and F1@0.5 (0.381 to 0.385).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are AI systems that process both images and text, often used for tasks like visual grounding where they must output bounding box coordinates. Fine-tuning with Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) is a popular method to adapt large models to specific tasks efficiently. The class-aware F1 score measures precision and recall per object class, while repeated-tail pressure refers to the tendency of models to generate repetitive outputs.

## [VISTA: View-Consistent Self-Verified Training for GUI Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14579) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VISTA introduces a GRPO-based training framework that constructs comparison groups from multiple target-preserving views of the same GUI instance, enabling meaningful relative advantage comparisons for GUI grounding. This work addresses a critical limitation in applying reinforcement learning to GUI grounding, where standard GRPO fails due to homogeneous rollouts, and consistently improves grounding accuracy across multiple benchmarks and model backbones. VISTA uses a self-verified cross-view anchor with an advantage-weighted loss, excluded from the group baseline and activated only when the model produces a maximum-reward rollout. On ScreenSpot-Pro, it raises Qwen3-VL 4B/8B/30B-A3B accuracy from 55.5/52.7/53.7 to 63.4/65.8/67.0.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: GUI grounding is the task of locating UI elements on a screen based on natural language instructions, crucial for GUI agents. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning method that compares rollouts within a group to compute advantages, but it struggles when all rollouts are equally successful or unsuccessful, as often happens with single-view GUI grounding.

## [SCSB: Sparse Bagging for Ensemble Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13589) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Simplex-Constrained Sparse Bagging (SCSB) is introduced as a post-training method that jointly prunes and calibrates bagging ensembles by optimizing over the probability simplex with a concave quadratic penalty to induce sparsity. SCSB achieves up to 96% ensemble compression with linear speedups and improved calibration, addressing the overconfidence and inefficiency of uniform-weight bagging ensembles like Random Forests. The method resolves the 'L1-simplex paradox' by using a concave quadratic penalty (−λ∥w∥₂²) instead of L1 regularization, which is constant on the simplex and cannot prune. SCSB is model-agnostic and uses Out-of-Bag predictions to avoid data leakage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Bagging ensembles like Random Forests combine multiple base estimators with equal voting weights. However, this uniform prior ignores the varying local competence of estimators and often leads to overconfident predictions. Traditional sparsity-inducing methods like L1 regularization fail on the probability simplex because the L1 norm is constant there.

This paper explores sparsity as an alternative to low-rank constraints.

This paper introduces a systematic XAI framework for RhythmFormer, adapting four attribution methods to its bi-level routing attention and proposing a skin coverage metric and a faithfulness coefficient (SaCo) adapted for rPPG regression. This work addresses the critical gap in rPPG XAI by providing quantitative faithfulness metrics and physiology-grounded validation, moving beyond qualitative heatmap inspection and enabling auditable evidence for clinical deployment of rPPG. The study reveals a multi-hop leakage effect under sparse top-k routing, where attention rollout and flow restore connections that individual refined-attention layers set to zero; Beyond Intuition mitigates this, achieving the highest median refined skin coverage (0.83) and faithfulness (F=0.92) on UBFC-rPPG.

This paper introduces metrics like skin coverage and SaCo to provide numerical evidence of spatial alignment and perturbation faithfulness.

This work provides crucial insights for practitioners selecting GeoFMs and designing downstream pipelines, showing that adaptation settings like decoder design can be as important as model choice, and motivates more representation-aware evaluation strategies. This paper rethinks backdoor adversarial unlearning as a continual learning problem and proposes BI-BAU, which leverages catastrophic forgetting and blind inversion to achieve complete backdoor removal. This work addresses the critical limitation of existing backdoor defenses that only provide superficial safety, offering a theoretically grounded method for complete backdoor unlearning, which could significantly improve the robustness of safety-tuned models. BI-BAU formulates adversarial example generation as a blind inversion problem solved via an EM algorithm within bi-level optimization, and extends to untargeted attacks and multi-modal contrastive learning.

Researchers propose a two-stage statistical framework that adapts the Implicit Association Test (IAT) to evaluate associative interference in LLMs, separating response compliance from task-consistent classification. Testing on Claude Sonnet-4, Gemini 2.5 Pro, and GPT-5 reveals varying interference effects across models and domains. This work addresses a key methodological flaw in bias evaluation by isolating true bias from refusal behavior, enabling more accurate assessment of LLM fairness. The findings show that associative interference is not universal and can be mitigated in modern models, guiding future bias mitigation efforts. The framework uses a forced-choice JSON format to reduce variability and a two-stage model to separate compliance (following the response format) from interference (task-consistency difference between congruent and incongruent conditions). Claude Sonnet-4 showed strong interference in Gender-Career (DeltaP = 0.086), while GPT-5 exhibited minimal interference across domains.

**Background**: The Implicit Association Test (IAT) is a psychological tool used to detect subconscious associations between concepts, often applied to measure implicit stereotypes in humans. This new framework explicitly models compliance to avoid that confound.

The paper proposes SLC (State-space Logit Correction), a method that uses empirical-Bayes shrinkage and Kalman smoothing to correct per-item logit bias in knowledge tracing models, recovering discriminative ability lost by standard post-hoc calibrators. This addresses an underexplored problem in educational data mining: standard calibrators like Platt scaling improve calibration but leave AUC unchanged, while SLC improves AUC across multiple datasets and backbones, potentially leading to more accurate student modeling. SLC converts binary observations to Gaussian pseudo-observations via Laplace/IRLS, applies empirical-Bayes shrinkage through a Kalman smoother, and fits an offset-Platt link. The method improves AUC on all four datasets and NLL on three, with advantages concentrated on sparse items.

AdaCSM introduces a routing-based expert mechanism for conditional specialization within a parametric survival modeling framework, and demonstrates improved predictive performance over state-of-the-art methods on multiple real-world clinical cohorts.

## [MoE Boosts Anti-Spoofing Robustness Against Unseen Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14639) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers transformed a self-supervised speech model into a Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architecture by replacing feed-forward blocks with multiple expert networks and a gating mechanism, achieving an 11.9% relative improvement in macro EER across 14 spoofing datasets. This work addresses a critical weakness in current anti-spoofing systems—poor generalization to unseen synthetic speech—by leveraging MoE's specialization ability, potentially enhancing security for voice authentication systems against evolving deepfake attacks. The MoE conversion was applied to selected encoder layers of a self-supervised model, with a layer-wise gating mechanism controlling expert activation. The approach reduced macro EER from 5.46% to 4.81% on 14 datasets, and the paper also analyzes architectural choices and expert activation behavior.

**Background**: Self-supervised speech models learn rich representations from raw audio without labeled data, often used as feature extractors for downstream tasks like anti-spoofing. ## [Semantic mapping reveals gaps in learner agency measurement and AI research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10881) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers analyzed over 14,000 publications using a semantic analysis pipeline to extract 8,954 definitions and 2,700 scale items, revealing three dimensions of learner agency and autonomy: task, person, and sociocultural. They found that existing scales systematically underrepresent the sociocultural dimension, and current generative AI research in education focuses narrowly on learning regulation and control. This study empirically quantifies the 'jingle-jangle fallacy' in educational psychology, highlighting a critical blind spot in both measurement tools and AI-mediated learning environments. The findings urge researchers and educators to adopt a more multidimensional view of learner agency, which could lead to more holistic educational interventions and AI systems. The semantic analysis pipeline processed definitions and scale items from over 14,000 publications, using natural language processing to cluster meanings into three dimensions. The study found that generative AI research in education predominantly addresses the task dimension (regulation and control), neglecting the person and sociocultural dimensions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: The 'jingle-jangle fallacy' refers to two related errors: a jingle fallacy occurs when different constructs are given the same name, while a jangle fallacy occurs when the same construct is given different names. This confusion has long hindered cumulative knowledge in psychology. The study uses semantic analysis, a computational method that extracts meaning from text based on word usage patterns, to clarify conceptual boundaries.

## [Genomic Priors Solve Cold Start in Personalized Health AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13556) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new Bayesian inference framework uses an individual's genomic profile as a personalized prior to distinguish constitutional from environmental physiological variation, addressing the cold-start problem in health AI where models require weeks of behavioral data before making accurate interpretations. This approach could enable personalized health AI systems to provide meaningful physiological interpretations from the very first measurement, accelerating digital health adoption and potentially improving early detection of health anomalies. The framework uses GWAS-derived effect sizes to compute a genomic prior G-hat, then decays it as behavioral data accumulate, transitioning from genome-dominated to empirical-baseline-dominated inference. It grades genomic priors by evidence strength, distinguishing robustly replicated anchors (e.g., FTO, FADS1/2, FKBP5) from contested candidate genes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Personalized health AI systems often suffer from a cold-start problem: they need weeks of individual behavioral data to distinguish what is normal for a person (constitutional variation) from deviations caused by environment or state. Bayesian inference provides a principled way to incorporate prior knowledge, and genomic profiles are stable, available at birth, and causally upstream of physiology, making them ideal priors.

Researchers introduce Fractured Sampling, a novel inference-time strategy that interpolates between full Chain-of-Thought (CoT) and solution-only sampling by truncating reasoning trajectories, reducing token costs while preserving accuracy. This work addresses a key bottleneck in deploying LLMs for latency-sensitive applications by significantly reducing token usage without sacrificing reasoning quality, potentially enabling more efficient and scalable LLM reasoning in real-world scenarios. Fractured Sampling explores three orthogonal axes: number of reasoning trajectories, number of final solutions per trajectory, and truncation depth of reasoning traces. Experiments on five benchmarks show it achieves superior accuracy-cost trade-offs, yielding log-linear scaling gains in Pass@k versus token budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting improves LLM reasoning by generating intermediate reasoning steps before the final answer. However, full CoT can be token-expensive, especially for long reasoning chains. Truncated CoT, which stops reasoning early and directly outputs the answer, often matches full CoT accuracy while using far fewer tokens, motivating the Fractured Sampling approach.

## [Sentinel: Attention Probing for Efficient LLM Context Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23277) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Sentinel introduces a lightweight probe that decodes attention patterns from a frozen LLM to identify which parts of the context are actually utilized, enabling up to 5x compression without dedicated training. This approach addresses a key bottleneck in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) by reducing long, noisy contexts, potentially improving efficiency and accuracy in real-world applications. Sentinel uses a 0.5B proxy model to probe attention heads, achieving competitive QA performance on LongBench compared to methods using 7B-scale models, and generalizes to Chinese and out-of-domain settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) often retrieves long contexts that contain noise, degrading LLM performance. Existing compression methods rely on heuristic relevance or supervised models, but Sentinel instead probes how the LLM actually uses context during inference, using a lightweight classifier on attention patterns.

## [ShapKAN: Shift-Invariant Pruning for Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01663) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose ShapKAN, a pruning framework that uses Shapley values to assess node importance in Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks (KANs) in a shift-invariant manner, overcoming the unreliability of magnitude-based pruning methods. This improves the interpretability and compression of KANs, making them more practical for resource-constrained environments and enhancing their advantage over traditional black-box neural networks. ShapKAN quantifies each node's actual contribution by averaging marginal contributions across all possible coalitions, ensuring consistent importance rankings regardless of input coordinate shifts. Experiments on synthetic and real-world datasets show it preserves true node importance while enabling effective network compression.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks (KANs) are a neural network architecture inspired by the Kolmogorov-Arnold representation theorem, where each weight is replaced by a learnable univariate function (often a spline). The Shapley value is a cooperative game theory concept that fairly distributes gains among players based on their average marginal contribution. In machine learning, it is used for feature attribution. Shift-invariance means that the output does not change when the input is shifted, which is important for robust pruning.

## [RAMAC: Risk-Aware Offline RL with Generative Actors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02695) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RAMAC introduces a model-free offline RL framework that combines expressive generative actors (e.g., diffusion models) with a distributional critic and optimizes a composite objective of Conditional Value-at-Risk (CVaR) and behavioral cloning (BC) to achieve risk-averse policies in multimodal scenarios. This work addresses a critical gap in risk-averse offline RL by enabling expressive generative policies without sacrificing safety, which is essential for deploying RL in safety-critical domains like autonomous driving and healthcare. RAMAC is modular and model-free, using a diffusion actor as the generative policy and a distributional critic to estimate CVaR. The paper provides an objective-level analysis showing that behavioral cloning regularization suppresses out-of-distribution actions and stabilizes CVaR optimization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Offline reinforcement learning learns policies from fixed datasets without environment interaction, but faces challenges from out-of-distribution actions that can cause catastrophic failures. Conditional Value-at-Risk (CVaR) is a risk measure that focuses on the worst-case tail of the return distribution, commonly used in finance and safety-critical systems. Behavioral cloning (BC) is a simple imitation learning method that directly mimics actions in the dataset, often used as a baseline in offline RL.

## [Chronological Thinking for Full-Duplex Spoken Dialogue](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05150) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Chronological Thinking, a mechanism that enables full-duplex spoken dialogue language models to engage in on-the-fly reasoning while listening, rather than remaining idle. This approach shifts from conventional Chain-of-Thought reasoning to a strictly causal, latency-free design tailored for streaming acoustic input. This innovation addresses a key limitation of current full-duplex systems, which waste listening time by predicting silence tokens, and brings AI interaction closer to human conversational behavior. If validated, it could significantly improve the responsiveness and quality of real-time spoken dialogue systems, impacting applications like voice assistants and customer service. Chronological Thinking has two key properties: it is strictly causal (updating hypotheses only from past audio) and introduces no additional latency (reasoning is amortized during the listening window). Experiments show consistent improvements in response quality and competitive performance on full-duplex interaction metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Full-duplex spoken dialogue systems allow simultaneous listening and speaking, enabling real-time interaction and handling of user barge-in. Current models often remain idle during listening by repeatedly predicting a silence token, which differs from human behavior where listeners engage in lightweight thinking. Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning is a prompting technique that guides LLMs to generate intermediate steps, but it is designed for text and not suitable for streaming audio without modifications.

A new arXiv paper argues that AI disclosure mandates in scholarship should be grounded in agent-integrity rather than output evaluation or reproducibility, proposing a documentation-adequacy framework with five transparency elements. This work challenges prevailing reproducibility-based approaches to AI transparency in ethics research, offering a novel philosophical foundation that could reshape how journals and institutions craft disclosure policies. The framework operationalizes Meaningful Human Control through five elements: declaration, navigation, documentation account, process documentation, and development records, demonstrated by the paper's own archived documentation.

A study with 69 professional translators found that only a subset (16.2%) could reliably identify AI-generated short stories, while many misclassified them, often preferring AI texts. This raises questions about the reliability of human detection of AI-generated content in professional settings, especially as AI tools like ChatGPT-4o become more prevalent. Low burstiness and narrative contradiction were the most reliable indicators of AI authorship, while grammatical accuracy and emotional tone often led to misclassification.

## [HyperPotter Uses Hypergraphs for Audio Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.05670) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HyperPotter introduces a hypergraph-based framework that captures high-order interactions (HOIs) in audio deepfake detection, using clustering-based hyperedges and class-aware prototype initialization. It achieves an average relative equal error rate reduction of 12.68% across 13 test sets. This work addresses a critical limitation of existing audio deepfake detectors that only model local or pairwise features, missing discriminative patterns from multiple feature components. By capturing high-order interactions, HyperPotter significantly improves detection accuracy and generalization across diverse scenarios, enhancing security against AI-generated audio threats. HyperPotter uses hypergraphs where hyperedges connect multiple vertices to model high-order relations, unlike standard graphs that only model pairwise edges. The method shows robustness limits under severe codec or channel distortion, indicating areas for future improvement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Audio deepfake detection aims to distinguish real human speech from AI-generated audio. Traditional methods rely on local temporal/spectral features or pairwise relations, but they fail to capture complex interactions among multiple features. Hypergraphs extend standard graphs by allowing edges (hyperedges) to connect any number of vertices, making them suitable for modeling high-order interactions in data.

TabKD introduces a data-free knowledge distillation method for tabular data that learns adaptive feature bins aligned with teacher decision boundaries and generates synthetic queries maximizing pairwise interaction coverage, achieving state-of-the-art student-teacher agreement in 14 out of 16 configurations across 4 benchmarks. This work addresses a critical gap in data-free distillation for tabular data, which is common in privacy-sensitive domains like healthcare and finance, by explicitly modeling feature interactions. Researchers used linear probing and activation patching to study how language models plan future constraints during generation, finding that only Gemma-3-27B exhibits a causal handoff mechanism where the causal driver of rhyme constraint shifts from the rhyme word to the line boundary around layer 30. The study tested Qwen3, Gemma-3, and Llama-3 at over ten scales using rhyming-couplet completion. Researchers propose SAFformer, a spiking transformer architecture that introduces active predictive filtering inspired by the brain's predictive coding mechanism, achieving 80.44% top-1 accuracy on ImageNet-1K with only 26.58M parameters and 5.88 mJ energy consumption. This work demonstrates that spiking transformers can achieve state-of-the-art accuracy while maintaining high energy efficiency, addressing a key limitation of passive reactive paradigms in processing redundant visual data. SAFformer incorporates a novel spiking active predictive filtering attention mechanism and a multi-scale gated feedforward network, establishing new state-of-the-art results on CIFAR-10/100 and CIFAR10-DVS benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Spiking Neural Networks (SNNs) are brain-inspired models that use discrete spikes for computation, offering high energy efficiency. Predictive coding theory suggests the brain actively predicts sensory input and processes only unexpected signals. SAFformer applies this principle to spiking transformers, enabling them to suppress predictable visual features and focus on salient information.

## [RPC: Relational Pattern Consistency for Category Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09420) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Relational Pattern Consistency (RPC), a method for Generalized Category Discovery that uses bidirectional knowledge transfer between labeled and unlabeled data via One-vs-All classifiers and relational pattern matching. RPC addresses a key limitation in existing GCD methods by explicitly coupling labeled and unlabeled data, enabling mutual enhancement and achieving state-of-the-art performance on both generic and fine-grained benchmarks. RPC uses One-vs-All classifiers for soft ID/OOD decomposition and introduces two mechanisms: semantic behavioral alignment for known-class preservation, and relational pattern matching for novel category discovery.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Generalized Category Discovery (GCD) aims to classify both known and novel categories from unlabeled data, given a set of labeled known classes. Existing methods often treat labeled and unlabeled data separately, missing opportunities for mutual improvement. Relational patterns refer to consistent interactions between samples and class prototypes, which RPC leverages to replace unreliable pseudo-labeling.

Researchers propose CoRe-MoE, a two-stage reinforcement learning framework that decouples gait generation from terrain adaptation using a mixture-of-experts architecture, enabling humanoid robots to smoothly transition between walking and running across diverse terrains. This work addresses a key challenge in humanoid locomotion—unifying gait transition and multi-terrain adaptation in a single policy—which is critical for deploying humanoid robots in real-world environments like stairs, slopes, and unstructured outdoor terrains. The framework first learns a stable base gait policy for walking and running with smooth transitions, then introduces a terrain-aware MoE branch trained with a contrastive objective to promote expert specialization. ## [MP3: Multi-Period Pattern Pre-training for ST Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13119) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose MP3, a plug-and-play pre-training plugin that learns multi-period patterns to distinguish temporal mirages in spatio-temporal forecasting, achieving 4.7% MAE and 5.0% RMSE reduction on average across five baselines and five datasets. This addresses a fundamental limitation of existing STGNNs—temporal mirage—and provides a generalizable plugin that consistently improves forecasting accuracy, which is critical for applications like transportation, climate, and energy. MP3 introduces multi-period temporal modeling via edge convolution, multi-period spatial modeling with a bottleneck project and global memory bank, and cross-period pattern interaction using a causality-enhanced Transformer. The plugin integrates seamlessly into existing STGNN backbones.

**Background**: Spatio-temporal forecasting predicts future values based on both spatial and temporal dependencies. Urban data often exhibits temporal mirage, where similar short-term inputs lead to different future trends. The proposed framework uses softmax-based sampling to weight recommendations based on the user's current emotional state, modeled in a two-dimensional energy-valence space.

<a id="item-271"></a>
## [FAConformer: Frequency-Aware CNN-Transformer for AAD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14120) ⭐️ 6.0/10

FAConformer introduces a frequency-aware CNN-Transformer framework for auditory attention decoding that explicitly integrates band-specific encoding and adaptive cross-band interaction, outperforming the state-of-the-art by 4.9% on two public datasets. This work advances neuro-steered hearing systems by more effectively exploiting frequency-domain EEG information, which could lead to more accurate and robust brain-controlled hearing aids for multi-speaker environments. FAConformer decomposes EEG into multiple frequency bands, processes each with an independent CNN-Transformer encoder, and fuses them via a frequency-aware attention module; it also introduces band-wise auxiliary supervision to prevent under-optimization of weak branches.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Auditory attention decoding (AAD) uses EEG signals to infer which speaker a listener is attending to in a multi-speaker environment, a key challenge for neuro-steered hearing aids. Traditional methods often rely on handcrafted multi-band features or simple concatenation, which may miss band-specific patterns and cross-band interactions. FAConformer addresses this by learning frequency-aware representations end-to-end.

<a id="item-272"></a>
## [Pix2Pix-Hybrid: Conditional GAN for Hajj Crowd Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14297) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose Pix2Pix-Hybrid (P2P-H), a conditional GAN that generates synthetic Hajj crowd images using multi-channel conditioning (edges, grayscale, density, time) and multi-scale PatchGAN discriminators, and release a synthetic dataset of 10,000 images (CrowdH) and an annotated mixed dataset (CrowdH-Mix-469). This work addresses the scarcity of annotated crowd images in the Hajj domain, enabling better training of crowd-counting models while preserving privacy. The synthetic data reduced mean absolute error across all five tested crowd-counting models, demonstrating practical utility for data augmentation in niche domains. The generator is conditioned on eight input channels encoding structural cues (edges and grayscale) and contextual attributes (crowd density and time of day). Two multi-scale PatchGAN discriminators operating at different resolutions capture detailed textures in dense scenes. The model was trained on 993 real Hajj frames from 60 public videos, with attributes derived automatically.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 15, 04:00

**Background**: Conditional GANs (cGANs) like Pix2Pix learn to translate input images to output images under guidance of conditioning information. PatchGAN discriminators classify image patches as real or fake, preserving high-frequency details. U-Net is a popular encoder-decoder architecture for image-to-image tasks. Crowd counting models estimate the number of people in an image, often using density maps.
